Tuesday, the 4th February, 2025 / 15 Magha, 1946 (Saka) 
 
The House met at eleven of the clock, 
MR. CHAIRMAN in the Chair. 
 
BIRTHDAY GREETINGS 
 
MR. CHAIRMAN:  Hon. Members, pleased to extend greetings to hon. Members of 
Parliament, Shri Neeraj Dangi, on his bi rthday today and Shrimati S. Phangnon 
Konyak on her birthday tomorrow.  Her birthday is tomorrow.  Advance greetings! 
 Shri Neeraj Dangi  is serving his first term in this august House since June, 
2020, representing the State of Rajasthan. And he is also from Rajasthan.  Shri Neeraj 
Dangi hails from an illustrious family. He is the son of late Shri Dinesh Dangi, a popular 
leader who served as a Minister in the Go vernment of Rajasthan. Hon. Members, I 
had the good fortune to be in the Rajast han Legislative Assembly alongside his 
illustrious father.   
   Shri Neeraj Dangi has been General Secretary of the Rajasthan Pradesh 
Congress Committee for nine years, from 2011 to 2020. His dedication to the greater 
good of society was evident in his pivotal role in the Tsunami relief efforts in the 
Andaman & Nicobar Islands, far away from  Rajasthan, in February, 2005.  He is 
married to Dr. Preeti Soni and the couple is blessed with son, Yuvan and daughter, 
Katyayani.  He is a promising political work er with deep commitmen t to social justice 
and public welfare. 
 Shrimati S. Phangnon Konyak  has been a Member of  this esteemed House 
since April, 2022, currently serving her fi rst term.  Her election to this House was 
historic.  She became the first woman from Nagaland ever to be elected to Rajya 
Sabha.  She is only the second woman from the State to be elected to either House of 
Parliament or to the State Assembly.  She made history again by serving on the Panel 
of Vice-Chairpersons, becoming the firs t woman from Nagaland to hold this 
distinguished position.   
 Shrimati Konyak’s commitment to co mmunity service is truly commendable. 
She has played a critical role in enrolling nu merous school dropouts in several villages 
in Nagaland, helping to shape their brighter  future.  Her efforts in raising awareness 
about HIV prevention have played a pivotal role in youth empowerment in Nagaland.  
Equally noteworthy is her dedication to pres erving the rich cultural heritage of the 
Konyak tribe. 

 
 
 Hon. Members, on my own behalf and on  behalf of this Ho use, I wish both 
hon. Members a happy, long and healthy lif e and extend greetings to their family 
members.

MATTERS RAISED WITH PERMISSION  
 
Concern over students facing difficulties in their job placements 
 
SHRI VIKRAMJIT SINGH SAHNEY (Punjab) : Hon. Chairman, Sir, IITs and NITs are 
the most prestigious institutions of the country and I would like to extend my gratitude 
to the hon. Finance Minister for adding 6,500  more seats in IITs in the Budget. But, 
unfortunately, Sir, it is a hard reality that  last year about 38 pe r cent IITians remain 
unplaced. As per the International Labo ur Organisation’s Report, educated 
unemployment in India was 65.7 per cent.  These numbers are really worrisome.  
Additionally, 83 per cent of the total unemployed people in India are young individuals.   
 Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, over 30 la kh posts are vacant across the Central 
and State Departments of the country. And, we all know that, last year, over one lakh 
youth applied for Sweeper post in Haryana. Si milarly, in UP, last year, for 411 Primary 
School Teacher vacancies, over ten lakh yo uth had applied!  If we speak about the 
private sector, the Indian IT sector, once a major job creator, has seen 40 per cent 
decline in the new hiring in 2024. 
 While self-employment and entrepreneur ship are critical solutions, there 
remains a huge gap between the policies on paper and actual delivery on ground. 
Schemes like PM Mudra Yojana and Startup India designed to provide collateral-free 
loans are often inaccessible due to stringent bank conditions and the youth don't get 
these loans without giving collateral.  Even it  is unfortunate to note that 83 per cent of 
loans given under Mudra Scheme were less than Rs. 50,000. I want to ask this 
House: Is it possible to start a Startup or dignified venture with Rs.50,000? Moreover, 
even education loans meant to empower youth require heavy collateral guarantees, 
making higher education a challenge for many  aspirants.  Even after spending about 
Rs.60 lakh to Rs.70 lakh, as per the estimate, from KG to PG, a youth does not get 
employment but a lot of unemployment and underemployment. Then adding to this, 
the rise of AI and automation poses a great threat. Studies pr edict that by 2040, 
nearly 70 per cent of the jobs in India's IT and service sector could be automated, 
displacing millions of workers.  To address this, we must focus on revitalising 
MSMEs, further boosting manufacturing under ‘Make in India’, create academic-
industrial linkages, enhancing vocational ed ucation from KG to PG. I will request the 
Leader of the House, Nadda ji, and the hon. Chairman to allow discussion on how to 
generate employment … (Time-bell rings)…     
                                                                          
6
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

Demand to expedite the construction of the overbridge near Siddha Mahaveer 
in Puri, Odisha 
 
SHRI SUBHASISH KHUNTIA (Odisha):  Hon. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I rise to draw the 
attention of this august House towards th e prolonged delay in the construction of 
overbridge near Siddha Mahaveer in Puri, which has caused severe inconvenience to 
the commuters and has disrupted connectivit y in the region.  This matter requires 
urgent intervention by the Government.   
 
Puri is globally renowned as the ab ode of Lord Shri Jagannath, a sacred 
pilgrim site and a prominent international to urism destination.  Lakhs of devotees and 
tourists visit Puri and nearby attractions such as the UNESCO World Heritage Site - 
Konark Sun Temple.   
 Puri-Konark Marine Drive Road, passing through Siddha Mahaveer, is a critical 
route for these visitors. However, the bridge  construction along this road has been 
progressing at a sluggish pace. The excavation of beach pillars has significantly 
narrowed the road, leading to severe tra ffic bottleneck and causing untold hardships 
to the commuters and the local residents.   
 Through you, Sir, I seek a response fr om the hon. Minister of Road Transport 
and Highways on the steps bein g taken to expedite this crit ical infrastructure project.  
I also request an update on the timelin e for its completion and urge upon the 
Government to implement interim measures to address the difficulties faced by the 
public, such as improving traffic management and providing alternative routes.  
8
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
 This road is the lifeline of Puri, supporting not only the mobility of its residents 
but also the region’s tourism-driven eco nomy. Any further delay would exacerbate 
the socio-economic impact. I propose that the Government establish a monitoring 
mechanism to oversee the time ly execution of such projects to prevent similar delays 
in future. I trust that the Minister would pr ioritise the matter and take swift remedial 
action. Jai Jagannath. Bande Utkala Janani.

Demand to declare Ganga Sagar Mela in Bengal as a National Fair 
 
SHRIMATI MAMATA THAKUR (West Bengal) : &Sir, the Gangasagar Mela is one of 
the largest spiritual gatherings in the world,  second in size only to the Kumbh Mela, 
yet it holds significant importance of its ow n. This annual fair takes place on Makar 
Sankranti, whereas the Kumbh Mela occurs every four years at designated locations, 
with one specific site hosting it every twelve years.  
 The long history of the Gangasagar Mela  is acknowledged in various historical 
accounts. This event is linked to the ancient trade routes of East India in the Indian 
Ocean, associated with Greco-Roman commerce. For Hindu devotees, the 
Gangasagar Mela is a vital festival of faith, imbued with deep astrological and 
mythological significance. Ancient Indi an texts, including the Ramayana, 
Mahabharata, and Kalidasa's Raghuvamsa, ma ke references to the fair, along with 
figures such as Kapil Muni and the island of Sagar Dwip.  
 Every year, hundreds of th ousands of devotees from across India and around 
the world gather to take a holy dip during this significant event. This year, more than 
one crore devotees visited the Ganga Sagar Mela, and this number is expected to 
continue rising. The State Government of  West Bengal has implemented numerous 
initiatives to ensure comfortable travel, accommodation, and safety for all pilgrims. 
Remarkably, the entire cost is covered by  the State Government, with no financial 
contribution from the Government of In dia. Given the uniqueness, significance, 
magnitude, and spiritual depth of the Ganga Sagar Mela, I urge the Central 
Government to grant it the status of a Na tional Mela. If a similar incident to the 
Kundcenter issue had occurred at Ganga Sa gar, multiple teams from the center 
would have already arrived in Bengal. 
                                         
& English translation of the original speech delivered in Bengali.

Demand for declaration of a National holiday on the birth anniversaries of  
Bhagwan Rushabhdev and Bhagwan Mahavir Ji 
 
DR. K. LAXMAN (Uttar Pradesh): Thank you, Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, for giving 
me this opportunity to raise the most se ntimental and emotive issue pertaining not 
only to the Jain community but also to  all the peace lovers of this country.  जैन धमर् क े 
पहले तीथȊकर का नाम भगवान ऋषभदेव था। उन्हȂ आिदनाथ भी कहा जाता है।  भगवान ऋषभदेव 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
13

 
 
को अÍटापद पवर्त यानी क ैलाश पवर्त से मोक्ष की ĢािÃत हुई थी।  जैन धमर् क े 24 तीथȊकरȗ मȂ से 5 
का जन्म पिवतर् अयोध्या मȂ हुआ था।  जैन धमर् क े आिखरी व 24वȂ तीथȊकर भगवान महावीर जी थे।  
तीथȊकर जो होते हȅ, वे संसार और सागर, जन्म मरण क े चकर् से मोक्ष तक का मागर् िदखाते ह ȅ।

भागवान ऋषभदेव जी और भगवान महावीर जी, these two exemplary and extraordinary 
figures, shaped the ethical and spiritual land scape of not only Jain
ism but also of the 
broader cultural fabric of our great nation.   
 Bhagwan Rishabhdev, the first Tirthankara,  is widely regarded as the founding 
figure of Jain philosophy. His teachings ar e based on the principles of non-violence 
means ahimsa and satya means truth. Bhagwan Mahavir ji, the 24 th Tirthankara, 
further refined and deepened these teachings which are integral to the path of spiritual 
enlightenment and peace. His life and teachin gs, cutting across religious boundaries, 
offer universal values that are relevant to the humanity. The Jain community for 
centuries has made immeasurable contributi on to the growth and progress of India 
whether in the field of business, trade, education, philanthropy and social welfare.  
Their efforts in promoting sustainability, ch arity and non-violence have played a vital 
role in shaping harmonious, prosperous and inclusive society. We have seen their 
yeoman service, particularly during the co rona period. We have seen how dedicated 
they are to the needs of society. In rec ognition of the contribution of these two 
profound spiritual figures, Bhagwan Rishabhdev ji and Bhagwan Mahavir ji, I urge 
upon the Government to declare their birth anniversaries as national holidays and see 
that the sentiments of not only the Jain community but of all th e peace-loving people 
are honoured. Thank you very much, Sir.

Demand to declare Bali Yatra as a National Festival 
 
SHRI DEBASHISH SAMANTARAY (Odisha) :  Come Kartik Purnima and along the 
banks of the Mahanadi, there are thousands of boats which are floated symbolically 
remembering the 2000-year maritime glory of Odisha.  
 This fair over the years has taken a ve ry big proportion. It has assumed such a 
size that people have called it one of the biggest fairs in Asia. Over a period of seven 
14
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
days, it has grown to a national festival  in many ways where you find national 
craftsmen, dance performances, weaving and self-help groups. Tribals participate in 
this fair and sell their produce. Our hon. ex -Chief Minister and my leader, Mr. Naveen 
Patnaik, had declared it a State function  and had requested the Central Government 
to accord it a national festival status.  
 Bali Yatra finds a mention in the speech  of our hon. Prime Minister, Shri 
Narendra Modi ji, when he visited Indonesia. He recalls this festival in Odisha which 
links India to Southeast Asia in Indonesia fo r thousands of years. This maritime travel 
was specifically for Bali, Sumatra, Java, Borneo, South-East Asia, Sri Lanka and 
Malaysia.  This year, the Government of India sent a delegation of Excellencies from 
all the embassies to visit this fair. As it  already finds mention in our hon. Prime 
Minister's speech in Indonesia, I request,  through you, Sir, to the Culture and 
Tourism Minister to declare this festival a national festival. I am sure if anyone 
amongst these colleagues of mine has visited Odisha at this particular time, he will 
remember an age-old ancient tradition whic h is still practised. Sir, I thank you and 
through you, I request the Culture and Tourism  Minister to immediately accord it the 
status of national festival.

Concern over the adverse impact of climate change on agriculture 
 
SHRI S. SELVAGANABATHY (Puducherry) : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, the global 
climate change has affected ev ery walk of life, especially affecting agriculture in many 
ways. The effect on agriculture is a seriou s problem since it is affecting the food 
security of the people. Till recently, rainfall patterns had been uniform and 
predictable. Therefore, farmers have been cultivating in a set pattern called Kharif and 
Rabi seasons. But, because of the erratic weather pattern caused by climate change, 
the rainfall pattern has completely chan ged. There are unseasonal downpours in 
unexpected dry periods. These have upse t the crop growth. Crops have been 
affected by unexpected heavy downpours and floods, causing great loss to the 
farmers. Often, the Government has to extend financial compensation to the farmers 
during such occasions. As a result, the food production declines and the Government 
16
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
suffers financial loss. There is a danger that  the gains of the Green Revolution may be 
lost. Warming can cause crops to develo p faster, producing less grains. Higher 
temperatures can also make it  hotter for plants to get unus ed moisture.  Research in 
agricultural sector has advanced greatly in India. Now, there is a need for addressing 
the problems of farmers suffering from climate change disasters.   
 In this connection, I have studied that  there are rice varieties which grow for 
over 10-15 feet in height in Indonesia.  Such varieties are also available in the 
traditional rice collection of India. I suggest that the agricultural institutes can examine 
the suitability of such flood tolerant va rieties and enhance their genetic capacity 
through hybridisation and release new high yi elding as well as flood tolerant varieties 
to farmers.  Alternately, technical advice should be given to agri culturists to change 
the cropping pattern suitably. The ill effe cts of El Nino and La Nina are more 
damaging than ever before. Climate change  can also increase water use leading to 
scarcity and also soil degradation.  
 Therefore, I request that necessary act ion should be taken by the Government 
to monitor the adverse impact of climate change on agriculture on war-footing. Thank 
you.   
                       
MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN : The following hon. Members a ssociated themselves with 
the matter raised by the hon. Member, Shri S. Selvaganabathy : Shri Maharaja 
Sanajaoba Leishemba (Manipur), Dr. Fauzia Khan (Maharashtra), Shri R. Girirajan 
(Tamil Nadu), Dr. Sasmit Patra (Odisha) , Shri Sadanand Mhalu Shet Tanavade 
(Goa), Shri Madan Rathore (Rajasthan), Dr. Kanimozhi NVN Somu (Tamil Nadu), 
Shri Jose K. Mani (Kerala), Shri Sujeet Kumar (Odisha), Shri Niranjan Bishi (Odisha) 
and Shri M. Mohamed Abdulla (Tamil Nadu).   
                       
Demand for establishment of AIIMS at Kinalur in Kozhikode, Kerala 
 
SHRIMATI P. T. USHA (Nominated) : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, let me take this 
opportunity to bring to your kind attention an utmost significant matter that will define 
the path of development of Kerala. My State,  Kerala, requires an institution offering 
advanced healthcare infrastructure system that matches the aspirations of the State in 
providing comprehensive healthcare for a ll, as our country is rapidly progressing 
during ‘Amrit Kaal’ era. 
 Kerala’s healthcare sector deserves an All India Institute of Medial Sciences to 
complement its existing advances made in healthcare sector. I feel that the 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
17

 
 
responsibility bestowed upon me by be ing a Member of Rajya Sabha must be 
optimally utilised by committing an AIIMS facility at Kinalur, Kerala.  
 Kinalur is the first choice of the Kerala  State Government for the proposed All 
India Institute of Medical Sciences. The adva ntages of the location are that a majority 
of the land is owned by the Government, minimising land acquisition process from 
private land owners and increasing the scop e of the future expansion of the AIIMS 
institute upon establishing. 
 At present, about 200 acres of land is required for the project and Kerala State 
Industrial Development Corporation handed over 153.46 acres of land. I have also 
given away 5 acres of land fr om my Usha School of Athl etics for the proposed AIIMS 
project.  The State Revenue Department has decided to acquire about 100 acres of 
land from private people.   
 Kinalur, being located in the pivotal point of the Malabar region of Kerala, is 
easily accessible for patients from districts like Nilgiris, Coimbatore (Tamil Nadu 
State) and Mysuru, Chamarajanagar, Kodagu (Karnataka State).  The entire Malabar 
region is connected to Kinalur with 6 kilo metres from the under-construction hill 
highway, 23 kilometres from National Highway 66, 10 kilometres away from the 
Koyilandy-Thamarassery-Edavannapara St ate Highway and 23 kilometres from 
Koyilandy railway station, 31 kilometres  from Kozhikode railway station and 53 
kilometres from airport, both Kannur and Kozhikode. The climate, environment and 
general ambience of Kinalur is conducive for the patients and aids in providing relief to 
them. 
 Therefore, I would request your good se lf and submit the vision of our hon. 
Prime Minister, Narendra Modi ji where healthcare is afford able for all and seek the 
establishment of an AIIMS facility at Kinalur, Kozhikode, Kerala.

SHRI R. GIRIRAJAN (Tamil Nadu) : Sir, the peak electricity demand in Tamil Nadu is

Fengal, a total of 12,265 electricity poles we re damaged, but they were replaced in 
record time.  Ahead of summer, the Ta mil Nadu Electricity Board (TNEB) and 
TANGEDCO officials have identified overlo ading issues in 6,536 transformers across 
the State, of which 5,407 have already been fixed. Sir, 48 new substations have been 
installed to strengthen the power grid along with the proposal to commission 20 
power substations throughout the State of Tamil Nadu to provide uninterrupted power 
supply. 
 According to the Load Generation Ba lance Report (LGBR) of the Southern 
Regional Power Committee (SRPC), Tamil Nadu’s  demand in April is expected to be 
20,600 MW, against the availability of 21,4 88 MW, resulting in a surplus of 985 MW.  
But, in the evening, the requirement would be 20,700 MW as against the availability of 
16,003 MW, leading to a deficit of 4,697 MW. 
 As per Economic Survey of India 2021, India’s Transmission and Distribution 
(T&D) losses have been over 20 per cent of  generation, which is more than twice 
the world average.  If only the Govern ment reduces the T&D losses, the power 
supply will see the brighter side.  Tamil Nadu being one of the top industrial States in 
the country, its power supply and demand too is high and the per capita consumption 
too is on a higher side.   
 Therefore, I urge the Union Government to take all  necessary steps to reduce 
the T&D losses and provide adequate financia l support to develop state of art power 
infrastructure in Tamil Nadu with a reduced rate of interest.  Thank you, Sir.

Need to enhance the potential of National Waterways 
 
MS. SUSHMITA DEV (West Bengal):  Thank you, Sir, for the opportunity.  Sir, I come

in the fact that we have the Brahmaputra and the Barak Rivers, whereas we often see

that riverways is actually one of the cheap est modes for movement of goods.  If we 
look at history of Assam, we share a huge border with Bangladesh, Myanmar etc.  
Our rivers are an asset through which we sh ould be facilitating movement of goods.  I 
feel lucky that Sarbananda Sonowal ji is present in Parliament today. And, in the 16th 
Lok Sabha, when I represented the Consti tuency of Silchar, the Barak River was 
declared a National Waterway. And, we we re all very hopeful and aspirational that 
once this waterway is properly made oper ational with proper infrastructure, a very 
remote and backward area lik e Barak Valley will be able to enhance its economy due 
to movements of goods in the Barak River. Unfortunately, Sir, infrastructure till today 
remains incomplete.  The dredging of Rive r Barak has been aban doned due to which 
the people of Barak Valley are almost isolat ed in the State of Assam. Trains become 
dysfunctional due to floods and landslid es because we are surrounded by Dima 
Hasao and Karbi Anglong. Airways are beyond the affordability of common man and 
small traders.   
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
25

facilitate the dredging of Barak River. Please facilitate the movement of goods through 
our riverways. We need ports to be made. We need proper customs infrastructure to 
be there so that  the otherwise isolated  area of Assam can gr ow faster, meet the 
aspirations of youth, boost our economy, connect us better for international trade and 
we can come out from the backwardness that we have been facing for decades.

manages the Brahmaputra River. But, till to day for the Barak river of Assam, we do 
not have a board that manages river prop erly. So, these are my demands in the 
greater interest of the people of Assam. Thank you, Sir.

ORAL ANSWERS TO QUESTIONS 
 
Pradhan Mantri Internship Scheme (PMIS) 2024-25  
 
*16.  SHRIMATI MAHUA MAJI : Will the Minister of CORPORATE AFFAIRS be pleased 
to state: 
(a) whether Government has prepared a detailed action plan to provide 
internship opportunities to one crore  youth under the Pradhan Mantri 
Internship Scheme (PMIS) announced in the Budget 2024-25, the details 
thereof; 
(b)  the steps being taken to partner with top 500 companies under this scheme; 
the details thereof; 
(c) whether this scheme would improve the employability skills and future 
employment opportunities of the youth, the details thereof; and 
(d)  the details of internships provided under this scheme, State-wise? 
 
THE MINISTER OF FINANCE (SRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN) :  (a) to (d) A 
Statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
 
Statement 
 
  (a) and (b) The Prime Minister’s Internship Scheme (PMIS) was announced 
in the Budget 2024-25. It aims to provide internship opportunities to one crore youth 
in top 500 companies in five years. As an  initiation to this Scheme, Ministry of 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
27

 
 
Corporate Affairs has launche d a Pilot Project of the Sc heme on 3rd October, 2024, 
which is targeted to provide 1.25 lakh internship opportunities to the youth in the 
Financial Year 2024-25. Guidelines containing  details for Prime Minister’s Internship 
Scheme - Pilot Project are available at https://pminternship.mca.gov.in. 
 The top 500 companies have been sele cted on the basis of the average 
Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) expenditure in the last 3 years. Apart from 
these, any other company/ bank/ financial in stitution desirous of participating in the 
Scheme, may do so with the approval of the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA), 
which would take a view keeping in view underrepresented sectors and areas in the 
above mentioned 500 companies. The list of the partner companies is available at 
https://pminternship.mca.gov.in. Participatio n of the companies in this scheme is 
voluntary. The Ministry is working in active collaboration with industry associations for 
implementation of the Pilot Project.  
  (c)   The Guidelines of the PM Intern ship Scheme Pilot Project provide that the 
duration of the Internship shall be 12 months, and at least half of the internship period 
must be spent in the actual working expe rience/ job environment, and not in the 
classroom. Thus, the PM Internship Scheme provides an opportunity to the youth to 
get training, gain experience and skills wi thin the real-life environment of the 
businesses or organizations that helps in bridging the gap between academic learning 
and industry requirements, in turn, assisting enhancement of her/his employability. 
  (d)  In Round I of the PM Internsh ip Scheme Pilot Project, about 1.27 lakh 
Internship Opportunities we re offered by the partner companies across the country. 
State-wise details of internship opportuni ties provided by Pa rtner Companies under 
the Round I of the PM Internship Scheme- Pilot Project are enclosed at Annexure.  
 
Annexure

SHRIMATI MAHUA MAJI : Sir, many Government Skill Development Programmes 
focus only on training but failed to ensure  employment. What concrete measures is 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
29

 
 
the Government taking under Pradhan Mantri  Internship Scheme to guarantee post-
internship job placements or further career opportunities?  
 
SHRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN : Sir, internship has a different aspect from 
employment. Internship is to give the youth who till now have not found an 
opportunity or because of their lack of having enough exposure and skill sets, the 
Government wanted to give them the o pportunity to get the internship or the 
exposure in the top 500 companies and, thro ugh that, with the exposure and with the 
internship having been comple ted, they will be in a better place to seek jobs. So, in 
the Budget of July, 2024, we came up with five different kinds of approaches-one to 
provide jobs, another to provide contribution  from the Centre to make sure that the 
employers who already have number of empl oyees can bring in additional employees 
where the Centre would give them support, both for employees and for employers. In 
those five approaches, one programme provides for internship and this scheme is 
meant for people who till then could not find  an opportunity because either they are 
not skilled enough or they are not traine d enough. Therefore, the intent of the 
programme itself is not to provide jobs bu t to provide an exposure and, through the 
internship, some kind of awareness of what is there in the market for which they have 
been trained. So, the intent itself was only  to provide exposure and, in a very loose 
sense, some kind of skilling within the organisations. It was a part of the 
Government’s attempt to provide jobs, at the same time, provide the readiness to 
those who are still not ready for the market. That was the intent of the scheme. 
 
SHRIMATI MAHUA MAJI : Sir, with history of human trafficking and gender-based 
vulnerabilities, what specific safeguards are being implemented to ensure the safety 
and security of female interns under Prad han Mantri Internship Scheme? And, will 
there be any special provisions or reserved slots to encourage higher participation of 
women in the scheme? 
 
SHRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN: Sir, I will take this opportunity to say, both at the 
selection or the short-listing by the Go vernment, through the website and also 
through the organisations themselves, picking up short-listed candidates, the 
Scheduled Castes, the Scheduled Tribes an d Other Backward Classes, as per the 
constitutional requirement, are being gi ven their due place. We are also actively 
encouraging women over and above these three categories to apply and, with a 
general sense of consideration, they ar e provided with an opportunity by the 
companies to locate them within their district so that they do not need to travel too far 
30
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
or making sure that if not within the distri ct, we are providing them different district 
within their State, and women have found it very useful. We have put out, through our 
handles, the experiences of young girl s who have come up and taken these 
opportunities to say what these opportunities are given to them. And, almost on a 
daily basis, we are putting out videos and small clips of intern s who are benefited, 
particularly, girls because that can encourage other women to come forward and take 
the opportunity. 
 
SHRIMATI MOUSAM B. NOOR : Mr. Chairman, Sir, I would like to request the hon. 
Minister to state whether it is true th at, despite setting a target of 1,25,000 
internships, only 10,000 internships have been provided under the PM Internship 
Scheme. If so, please explain why less than 10 per cent of the targeted internships 
have been provided and outline the Government's plan to address this issue. 
 
SHRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, one of the pilot 
projects, which commenced last year, had th e industry offer of 1.27 lakh positions, 
providing opportunities to potential candi dates. Then, potential candidates were 
asked to apply. And from then on, they we re given the opportunity to choose within 
their district. Just as I explained a minute ago,  if the opportunity is available within the 
district and they prefer to be there, they  take it; otherwise, they move to another 
district where they want to go.  So, in the process of taking the final offer - not giving 
the final offer, but the candidate taking  the final offer - time lapses, and the 
opportunities and offers remain available.  So, they are moving. Additionally, some 
companies are offering extra benefits, such  as lunch or accommodation if they come 
from a different district, although this is  not part of the tailored scheme. Companies 
themselves have come forward.  So, wh en candidates get to know about these 
offers, they also want to change or move to   a company    that provides   a   better 
offer within their district. So, in this proce ss, there is an evolving side of the story 
where the offers remain, and the students or candidates who come forward are trying 
to find their suitable choice. Even as this  is happening with the first pilot, which 
started last year, the second pilot has commenced from January. Similarly, nearly 80 
more companies have come forward with more offers. I am glad to say that among all 
the districts in the country, we have placed young people in various positions in nearly 
743 districts. So, it is an evolving situatio n, and I am glad that within the first four 
months, after announcing the July Budget, we have received this kind of response. 
 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
31

MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN:  Now, Question No. 17. 
 
Procurement of paddy and wheat at MSP 
 
#*17. SHRI UPENDRA KUSHWAHA:  Will the Minister of CONSUMER AFFAIRS, 
FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION be pleased to state:   
      (a)  the target for procurement of  paddy and wheat at Minimum Support Price  
(MSP) in the last  three  years  along  with  their  quantities  procured  
corresponding  to  the target, the details thereof, State-wise; and  
       (b)  the amount  sp ent  on  the  procurement of the aforementioned food grains 
in  the last three years, the details thereof, State-wise? 
 
THE MINISTER OF CONSUMER AFFAIRS,  FOOD AND PUBLIC DISTRIBUTION 
(SHRI PRALHAD JOSHI): (a) and (b) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
                                         
#Original notice of the question was received in Hindi. 
 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
33

 
 
Statement 
 
  (a) The estimates for procurement of Paddy and wheat are fixed during Food 
Secretaries’ meeting in consultation with State Governments concerned, Ministry of 
Agriculture and Food Corporation of In dia, before the commencement of each 
procurement season based upon estimate d production, marketable surplus and 
agricultural crop pattern.  
 
Key Initiatives:  
 
The improvements in paddy and wheat procur ement are the result of several farmer-
friendly initiatives undertaken by the Government of India:  
 
1.  The Government has consistently ensured MSP calculations are based on 
scientific principles, making cultivation profitable for farmers. Government of 
India in its Union Budget for 2018-19 announced to keep MSP at a level of one 
and half times of the cost of produc tion, as a pre-determined principle. 
Accordingly, MSPs for all mandated Kh arif, Rabi and other commercial crops 
including paddy, wheat and coarse grains have been fixed with a margin of at 
least 50 per cent over all India weighted average cost of production since 2018-
19.  
2.  Extensive outreach programs are conduc ted to ensure that farmers across the 
country are well-informed about procurement processes and MSP benefits.  
3.  Farmer’s registration is streamlined to minimize delays and improve 
transparency in procurement.  
4.  Investments in storage, transpor tation, and procurement centers have 
expedited procurement processes and reduced post-harvest losses.  
5.  Digitalization and direct benefit transfers have ensured that farmers receive their 
payments promptly.  
6.  The Hon’ble Minister has conducted a review with wheat-procuring states to 
encourage them to enhance wheat procurement efforts.  
Due to all these efforts the total proc urement of Paddy was 782.25 LMT during 
KMS 2023-24 and procurement of wheat was 266.05 LMT during RMS 2024-25.  
 
The State-wise procurement estimates and quantity procured for paddy and wheat at 
Minimum Support Price (MSP) in last three years is at Annexure-I & Annexure-II  
respectively.  
34
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
 
        (b) Procurement of paddy and wheat for central pool is carried out under 
Decentralized Procurement (DCP) system and Centralized procurement (Non-DCP) 
system. 
In the DCP system, State Governments an d their agencies procure, store and 
distribute wheat and paddy/rice  under National Food Security Act (NFSA) and Other 
Welfare Scheme (OWS) within the State. The excess stocks procured by the State 
and its Agencies are handed over to Food Corporation of India (FCI) in Central Pool. 
As regards the excess stock handed over by  the State to FCI, payment are released 
to the respective state by FCI which is reimbursed by Government of India to FCI. 
Government of India reimburses the expenditure incurred by DCP State for 
distribution of procured quantity within the state under NFSA and OWS.   
In Non-DCP system, the procurement of wheat and paddy/rice in Central Pool 
is undertaken either by FCI directly or  by State Government Agencies (SGA). 
Procurement carried out by SG A is handed over to FCI for storage and subsequent 
issue against Government of India allocations  in the same State or for movement of 
surplus stocks to other States. Governme nt of India reimburses the expenditure 
incurred by FCI on procurement of stock on its own as well as for stocks taken over 
from SGA.   
  The Details of fund released to DCP St ates during the last three financial years 
are as under:

 Impact of NSS interest rate reduction on retirees 
 
*18. DR. MEDHA VISHRAM KULKARNI : Will the Minister of FINANCE be please to
 
state:- 
(a) whether Government is aware that cu tting the National Savings Scheme 
(NSS) interest rate to zero will sign ificantly impact financial stability of 
retirees; and  
(b) if so, the details of immediate steps Government will take to mitigate the 
financial hardship caused by this su dden policy change, particularly for 
retirees who depend on this scheme for their income? 
 
THE MINISTER OF FINANCE (S HRIMATI NIRMALA SITHARAMAN) : (a) to (b) A 
statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
 
 
Statement 
  
(a) and (b) The NSS-87 and NSS-92 schemes were introduced on 01.04.1987 
& 01.10.1992 without any sunset clause. The total active accounts under all Small 
Savings Schemes are 39.92 crore, of which the accounts under NSS-87 & NSS-92 
are also at 1.37 lakh & 29,000 respectivel y. Fresh deposit under NSS-87 & NSS-92 
44
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
were discontinued from 01.10.1992 & 01. 11.2002, respectively. The interest rate 
applicable under these schemes with effect from 01.03.2003 is 7.5%. 
Currently, there are several ongoing sm all savings schemes which provides 
avenues of risk-free higher returns to investors e.g. Senior Citizen Savings Scheme 
with Interest rate 8.2%, Sukanya Samriddhi Account Scheme with Interest rate 
8.2% and Mahila Savings Certificate Scheme  with Interest rate 7.5%, etc. These 
could be availed for better returns. 
Further, necessary action to safeguard th e interests of investors has already 
been taken in Budget FY 2025-26.

MR. DEPUTY CHAIRMAN:  Question No.19. 
 
Preparation against future pandemics in the country 
 
*19. DR. KANIMOZHI NVN SOMU : Will the MINISTER OF HEALTH AND FAMILY 
WELFARE be pleased to state:-  
      (a) the measures/steps taken/proposed to be taken by Government to prepare 
against future pandemics in the country;  
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
47

 
 
(b) the details of financial assistance extended to the States including Tamil Nadu  
in tackling pandemics; and  
(c) the details of the outbreak of dis eases reported across the country including 
Tamil Nadu during the last two years? 
  
THE MINISTER OF STATE IN THE MINISTRY OF HEALTH AND FAMILY WELFARE 
(SHRI PRATAPRAO JADHAV) : (a) to (c) A Statement is laid on the Table of the 
House. 
 
Statement  
 
           (a) to (c) Prevention of an d preparedness against pandemics is shared global 
responsibility. In order to better prepare against future pandemics/public health 
emergencies in the country, Union Ministry of Health & Family Welfare provides 
requisite support to States/UTs to enhance their capabilities. 
 To enhance disease surveillance activitie s, the Ministry has strengthened the 
Integrated Disease Surveillance Programme (I DSP), which allows for a decentralized 
system of response through trained multi- disciplinary Rapid Response Team (RRTs) 
to institute requisite public health cont rol and containment measures. IDSP, through 
its Integrated Health Information Platform (IHIP) has also been strengthened to use 
advanced data modelling and data analytical  tools, which has features for real-time 
data reporting and is accessible at all levels.  
 In terms of laboratory strengthening, under IDSP, States have strengthened 
laboratories at district and State levels. Further, Indian Council of Medical Research 
(ICMR) has established a network of more than 150 Virus Research and Diagnostic 
Laboratories (VRDLs) for strengthening of nation-wide network of laboratories for 
timely laboratory based diagnosis of pathogens. National Institute of Virology (NIV), 
Pune is the apex laboratory for detectio n and confirmation of pathogens. With the 
view to strengthen the diagnostic capacities for bacteria, fungi and parasites, some 
VRDLs have been identified for upgradat ion as Infectious Disease Research and 
Diagnostic Laboratories. ICMR has develo ped two mobile Biosafety Level 3 (BSL-3) 
laboratories to provide essential on-sit e diagnostic services during outbreaks, 
especially in remote areas. 
 To undertake integrated and holistic research and development across human, 
animal, plant, and environmental health sect ors, a National Institute for One Health 
has been approved in Nagpur, Maharashtra. 
48
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
 With the long-term goal to better pr epare our country against public health 
emergencies, Pradhan Mantri - Ayushman Bh arat Health Infrastructure Mission (PM-
ABHIM) has been launched with the intent  to enhance the capacity of primary, 
secondary and tertiary health care facilities/systems and institutes for identifying and 
managing any new and emerging diseases. 
 Funding support has been provided to States/UTs for health system 
strengthening to meet any exigency due to public health emergencies like pandemics. 
During the FY 2020-21, funds to the tune of  Rs. 8473.73 crore have been released to 
the States/UTs towards the India COVID-19 Emergency Response and Health System 
Preparedness Package (ECRP-I) for health infrastructure strengthening, expansion 
of laboratory network, surveillance, procurement of medical logistics etc. Under India 
COVID 19 Emergency Response and Heal th Systems Preparedness Package-Phase 
II, a sum of Rs. 12,740.22 crore support was provided to States/UTs for ramping up 
health infrastructure and provision of me dical logistics. Further, a release of 
approximately Rs. 4791.67 crore has been provided as central release of grants under 
PM-ABHIM (since April 2021-22 till date) fo r better public health preparedness 
through Critical Care Blocks at Medical Co lleges and District Hospitals, Integrated 
Public Health Laboratories, etc.  
 Financial support of Rs. 893.74 crore,  Rs. 479.59 crore and Rs. 615.95 crore 
was provided to State Government of Ta mil Nadu under ECRP-I, ECRP-II and PM-
ABHIM respectively.  
 As per information provided by States/U Ts to IDSP, 1,862 incidents of disease 
outbreaks such as Acute Diarrhoeal Disease, Food poisoning, Dengue etc. have been 
reported in 2023 and 2,947 incidents have been reported  in 2024 (till 15 December, 
2024). Of these, Tamil Nadu has reported 61 such incidents in 2023 and 195 in 2024 
(till 15
th December, 2024).

emergency witnessed in the recent years.  Ar e we ready to tackle any such pandemic 
in the future is a billion dollar question.  To  take effective timely action, a pandemic 
preparedness and emergency response fund  needs to be established. The PM 
CARES Fund was registered as a public char itable trust under the Registration Act, 
1908, on March 7
th, 2020, aimed to create a financ ial reserve to address health 
emergencies.  And it is still receiving dona tions. I would like to know from the hon. 
Minister, through you, the total donation s received and spent till date, and steps 
taken by the Government to pass new legislation and to formulate internationally 
legally binding rules and regulations.   
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
49

 
 
 
Ǜी Ģतापराव जाधव: उपसभापित महोदय, मȅ आपके  माध्यम से बताना चाहूंगा िक महामारी की

SHRI JAGAT PRAKASH NADDA :  Sir, it is an opportunity to explain to the hon. 
Members and the House itself that as far as emerging pandemics are concerned, we 
have got a very robust system. We have got the National Centre for Disease Control.  
It oversees and keeps surveillance over such  diseases. It is the Apex body which 
takes care of the diseases, the pathogens and other emerging virus mutation which 
takes place.  Under the NCDC, we have got an integrated disease surveillance 
programme, a platform for disease surveill ance, and we have got Rapid Response 
Teams (RRTs) which are there in the States. We have got decentralised 
multidisciplinary teams for health control and containment. We have got an integrated 
health information platform where we have ad vanced data with analysis and analytical 
data and data modelling tools. We give a real-time reporting for all the emerging 
pandemics or where there are chances of having any pandemic. The Indian Council 
for Medical Research has got 150 labs. We  have got viral research diagnostic 
laboratories.  Ten viral research diagnostic la boratories are there in
  Tamil Nadu itself.  
We have got the world-class National Institut e of Virology in Pune. The biosafety level 
is four. We have got two BSL-3 labs in  Tamil Nadu itself. We have got other 
diagnostic facilities.  We are upgrading the vi rology labs to BSL-3 levels.  So we have

51

 
 
got a very robust system. Wherever any pa ndemic or any chances of pandemics are

of medicine or what type of treatment is to be given. That is one thing. He mentioned 
about emerging pandemics. Prime Minister Modi has taken one step forward and now 
we have got National One Health Mission, NOHM.  This National One Health Mission 
includes many things because health is at the receiving end. We get diseases 
because of pesticides; we get diseases b ecause of environment; we get diseases 
because of the nature of the plants which is changing; we get diseases because of 
the medicines which are being given to the animals. What is the impact altogether? 
So, for that, we have also started the National One Health Mission which has been 
started by Prime Minister Modi at Nagpur with a cost of Rs.112 crore. We also had a 
test which we call as Visha nu Yuddh Abhyas. That was done  in Ajmer. It was a mock 
test just to see whether our health system is prepared for it or not. So, we are very 
vigilant on that account.

have taken place in Tamil Nadu and we continuously support them.

AYUSH visa for availing treatment 
 
*20. SHRIMATI KIRAN CHOUDHRY: Will the Minister of AYUSH be pleased to state: 
(a)  the details of the maximum duration  of stay allowed under the Ayush Visa, 
and whether it can be extended based on treatment needs; 
(b) whether there are any fi nancial guarantees or insurance requirements for 
foreigners applying for this visa; and 
(c)   if so, the details thereof? 
 
THE MINISTER OF STATE OF THE MINISTRY OF AYUSH (SHRI PRATAPRAO 
JADHAV): (a) to (c) A statement is laid on the Table of the House. 
 
Statement  
 
  (a) The period of validity of Ayush visa is up to a period of one year or till the 
period of treatment, whichever is less with maximum of three entries during the one 
year period. Further, it can be extended on yearly basis up to a maximum period of 05 
years from the date of issue of initial Ay ush Visa by the concerned Foreign Regional 
Registration Office (FRRO) on production of Medical Certificate/ advice from a 
recognized hospital in India. 
 
  (b) and (c) An Ayush visa is granted to a foreigner who is holding sufficient 
money to spend during his/her stay in India including the expenses for such 
treatment. The concerned Mission/Post shall satisfy itself before granting such visa to 
the foreign national. However, there is no insurance requirement for applying Ayush 
Visa.

the same duration as the patient?

Trinamool Congress, I rise to speak in response to the hon. President’s Address.  My 
comments are, in no way, directed at the hon. President herself; I speak in opposition 
to the Government.  Sir, I would like to qu ote these lines from the renowned Bengal 
writer and poet, Sukumar Ray, from the famous collection Abol Tabol published in 
1923. & “Why is it that the Raja of Bombagorer  hangs mango pancakes in a painting, 
the queen wears a pillow on her head and the queen's brother hammers nails into 
slices of bread? ” This is a satirical poem. It shows that the Government is 
disconnected from reality.  It is totally disconnected. The citizen is forced to become a 
mute spectator, mutely watching the Govern ment's absurd drama. This is exactly 
what this Government is all about. This is a Government disconnected from reality. 
This is a Government that does not kn ow how to tackle the catastrophe of 
unemployment, the crisis of pr ice rise. This is a Government that does not know how 
to tackle the challenge of security in Manipu r. This is a Government not guided by our 
great national motto, ‘ Satyameva Jayate: let truth triumph’. This Government is not 
guided by Satyameva Jayate . Like the Raja of Bombagorer, this Government lives in 
an imagined reality. All it wants is media publicity and to set up a media narrative. This 
is precisely why India faces today an acu te governance deficit. This Government 
came to power with the slogan ‘Minimum Government, Maximum Governance’. 
Instead today, we have a Government of 'Minimum Governance, Maximum Publicity'. 
It is 'Minimum Governance,  Maximum Publicity'. I will give some examples of 
'Minimum Governance, Maximum Publicity'.  
 Sir, let us start with the Kumbh Mela, a classic example of 'Minimum 
Governance, Maximum Publicity'. The public ity blitz and event management at this 
year's Kumbh is so overpowering that it is as if there has never been a Kumbh mela 
before. I was a journalist. I covered the Kumbh mela in 2001 and 2013. There was 
much less hype, much more orderly crowd management, much better crowd 
management. This time, there has been this tragic stampede. More and more 
evidence is emerging, that, It is not just  one stampede. There are two stampedes, 
maybe three stampedes. Investigative journalists are reporting far higher number of

SHRI MANAS RA
NJAN MANGARAJ (Odisha) : & Respected Vice-Chairman, Sir, 
thank you for allowing  me to speak on the President's Address. However, it must be 
said with great indignation that while th e words spoken by the Central Government 
through the President sound pleasant, in reality, they are meaningless. In her 
address, the President spoke about increas ing farmers' income. Yet, farmers across 
the country, including those in Odisha, are still not receiving the MSP recommended 
by the Swaminathan Committee report. Just last month, 15 farmers in Odisha lost 
their lives within two weeks due to crop damage caused by unseasonal rains. The 
hon. President is the daughter of Odisha;  yet I did not sense any special concern for 
Odisha in her speech.  It has been stated that 3 crore families have been provided 
houses under the Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana. However, Odisha has been 
completely neglected in this regard. Even today, thousands of poor Scheduled Caste 
families in the KBK region remain deprived  of pucca houses. Five  years after Cyclone 
Fani, thousands of affected fa milies in the undivided Puri district are still waiting for 
the Central Government's mercy to secure a roof over their heads. 
 The Central Government claims to be committed to expanding the railway 
network from Kashmir to Kanyakumari.  Yet even after 75 years of independence, 
many districts in Odisha have never seen a railway line. The Talcher-Bimalagarh and 
Khordha-Balangir railway projects have been stalled for two decades. The Jeypore-
Malkangiri and Jeypore-Nabarangpur railway lines remain in limbo despite the Naveen

SHRI K.R. SURESH REDDY (Telangana) : Mr. Deputy Chairman, Sir, I thank you for 
the opportunity given to me. I also express my gratitude to our respected 
Rashtrapatiji’s Address, which has strengthened th e confidence of this House.  Sir, 
the Address of Rashtrapatiji began with, and, very rightl y so, reminding us of the 75 th 
anniversary of the adoption of our Constituti on and also of celebration of 75 years of 
our Republic. Through her, the entire nation appreciates the contribution of Baba 
Sahib Ambedkar and the framers of the Consti tution.  While I take this opportunity to 
congratulate our Prime Minister, Modiji, and his Government for their achievements in 
the last many years, but there is a lot more to be desired or to be fulfilled.  Being the 
third term, I expected some major announcem ents through the President’s Address.  
When we pay tribute to Baba Saheb Ambe dkar, the first thing which comes to my 
mind, when I think of Baba Saheb Ambedkar , his Vision for the country, is water, 
पानी। Very rightly, he was called the waterman of our country because he had a vision 
of utilizing the water resources of our country. He was instrumental in creating many 
Boards which would direct the country, ad vise the country on water utilization. The 
fallout of that one could be the Central Water Commission. But, unfortunately, in the 
last 75 years, when we look at the utilizatio n of our water resources, there is a lot to 
be done, in spite of the fact that variou s Governments, through the initiation, have 
utilized the river waters to a certain extent but not to the desired level.  
 My leader and President of  my Party, KCRji, had a very clear vision. During the 
struggle for Telangana; and in the 10 years of his governance and subsequently even 
when we are out of power, he always kept reminding the people in power in Delhi the 
need to utilize and strengthen the water resources. He came out with facts, some 
very startling figures, not his figures, but given by the CWC.  
 In this country, Sir, every year, ther e is a rainfall of 1,40,000 TMC of water; 
1,40,000 TMC of water is in the form of rain fall. Assuming that 50 per cent of it, which 
is, say, 70,000 TMC, is absorbed by the ea rth, by the ground, there is still 70,000 
TMC of water which is available to be utilized . In the last 75 years, when we look at 
the figures, we get to know that only  20,000 TMC of water is utilized and the 
remaining 50,000 goes wasted, goes into the sea. Now 75 years to utilize 20,000 TMC 
of water! A country which is rich in resour ces, bountiful in rivers, a weather which is 
favourable for agriculture and various climat ic conditions which suit various crops, 
still, today what we face? We face water wars, inter-State disputes, intra-State 
disputes, inter-district disputes, intra-district disputes, in spite of God being so kind 
and giving us so much water! So what is the reason behind this? What are the 
causes for all this? What has the Government got to do? When I really look at it, and 
as my leader, KCRji, keeps rightly advising us, the fact is that whenever there is a 
dispute, as we now have, through an Act of Parliament, the Inter-State Dispute Water 
88
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
Tribunals. But when we look at the functioning of the tribunals, each tribunal has a list 
of tribunals in my own State, my neighbouri ng States, on the Godavari River, on the 
Krishna River; and on the rivers across th e country, there are various tribunals which 
have been functioning and functioning. If  you look at the average span of each 
tribunal to decide, it is more than 10 years, 15 years, 20 years that  they are taking to 
resolve a dispute. What happens in this period of time? What happens is, a 
generation or two generations are lost. गरीब वहीं का वहीं रह गया। And this is a loss in a 
country which has lost generations fighting for freedom; and after freedom, in the 75 
years, we are still not able to resolve this  fundamental natural resource which is so 
essential for our prosperity.  
 Today in the august House when hon.  Members were speaking of the divide 
between the haves and have-nots, the ineq uality levels growing, the rural-urban 
divide, what are the ways and means to fix this? The Government is doing a few 
things, but according to Baba Saheb Am bedkar, one magic for the country is 
utilization of our water resour ces. A fitting tribute today in  the 75th year when we pay 
our tribute and when we honour him, I hope  and pray and I hope to hear when the 
Prime Minister replies to the august House on  the Motion of Thanks that he will come 
with an announcement on the utilization of the river water. 
 There is need for the Central Governme nt to ensure that the inter-State 
disputes are settled at the earliest.  There is a need for the Government of India to 
intervene, bring the concerned parties and St ates to the Table an d when issues are 
resolved, some form of an incentive coul d be given.  When dams are planned, a 
Central grant could be given; the Centre coul d give 20, 30 or 40 pe r cent as grant.  In 
this Budget, you announced a major initiati ve, a dam for Bihar.  Similar initiatives 
could be taken for all the corners of the count ry.  Sir, I was really taken aback when I 
saw the kind of storage capacities we have  in our dams.  The biggest dam in our 
country, which is in Madhya Pradesh, the Indira Sagar Dam, has a capacity of 431 
TMC. When you look at the average rainfall  in Madhya Pradesh, it is about 12,778 
TMC. So, this is the quantum of rainfall, and the storage capacity of the reservoir is at 
431 TMC. Comparing it globally, let us look at a small country like Zambia or 
Zimbabwe. There they have this Kariba Dam,  which stores 6,376 TMC of water.  The 
entire waterfall of Madhya Pradesh is stored in one reservoir there.  I can, of course, 
go on and on with data about various dams  here and across the globe.  There is a 
big, big difference.  Now, if we wish to build a large dam, there comes the question of 
resettlement.  That becomes an issue.  How do we resolve this?  Well, Telangana is a 
very good example. We have built the wo rld’s largest lift irrigation dam, the 
Kaleshwaram Lift Irrigation Project, which has transformed Telang ana in a matter of 
six years, under the leadership of KCRgaru , from a drought-stri cken area to the 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
89

 
 
number one paddy growing State in the co untry. So, land acquisition was not a 
problem; technology is available; a series of dams, a series of lifts, ensured that the 
major tracts of water were brought under irri gation. So, these are the initiatives which 
need to be taken up. 
 The second thing which is troubling us  is power. Now, power is also a big 
issue. When you look at power, there is ab undant electricity available in the country.  
For example, the installed capacity of our na tion is 462 GigaWatts.  And, what is the 
peak utilization?  It is only 250 GW.  So, there is an abundance of power which is not 
being utilized. The reasons for that could be many.  One could be transmission lines, 
the other could be disputes. But all said and done, when the capacity is available and 
there are regions which are not getting power, why can’t some out-of-the-box 
thinking be done to resolve th ese matters?  We are in the 75 th year of our Republic.   
Sir, these are the concerns that are really a ffecting the growth of our nation.  I believe 
that in the coming months and years, if areas like these are addressed, then, 
definitely, the progress that you are lookin g at, of achieving a five or seven trillion 
dollar economy, could be achieved and equitable distribution of wealth could take 
place, which is so paramount for a nation as diverse as India.  Also, harmony could 
be maintained in the interest of our country. 
 Sir, if we look at the recent happenings of the Kumbh Mela, it has been an eye 
opener.  Of course, I express my condolences to the families that have lost their loved 
ones.  It was a huge congregation. In spite of the Government of Uttar Pradesh doing 
its best, unfortunate incide nts happened. But my suggestion to the Government is 
this.  India has got such a vast talent. You have IAS, IPS and top-notch officers all 
over the country.  There are regular pass-outs coming out of the Sardar Patel Training 
Institute.  When such a big event takes place, why don’t you put these officers there?  
Like the Election Commission picks them up  and deputes them as observers across 
the nation, why not assemble such people  there too?  Two things can happen -- 
one, there would be proper regulation of traffic, proper monitoring, and two, for these 
young officers, it would be an experience.  The kind of diversity and diverse people 
that they get to see and interact, it woul d be a good learning ex perience for them as 
they are the future administrators.  They  would be in the PMO and CMO and guiding 
people in the future.  If th ese are the things which can be taken up, it would be a very 
nice thing.  It is never too late.  You have another 20 days to go for the Kumbh Mela.  
Also, in the coming years, we would have the Godavari Pushkaralu and other similar 
things coming up in the country. 
 So, learn from Kumbh and ensure that those things do not happen. North-
East has been a region where I like to co ngratulate the Government that they have 
done remarkably good work.  For the last many years, North-East has come into 
90
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
prominence. North-East has become a very favourable destination for many. But in 
North-East, Manipur stands out. On Manipu r, I would like to reiterate my request. 
Since this is the President's Ad dress, I wish an Address by Rashtrapati ji  or even 
Uparastrapati ji to Manipur, If they can address or she can address the House, she 
will be a catalyst. It will be above politics. It will be a healing touch for the people of 
Manipur. If this can be taken by the Government, I think, it would go a long way in 
solving not only Manipur problem, but also putting up the North-East corridor entirely 
in the national grid.   
 Initially, when I came to speak, it was only five minutes allotted to me. So, I 
said, ‘I have four Members and I am the only speaker. How can you give me five 
minutes?’ You were kind enough that you ga ve me 15 minutes. I hope this tradition 
continues, that the tradition also continues for the Budget and other occasions.   
 Finally, I would like to speak on anti -defection.  You are a very passionate 
speaker.  You are passionate about speaking not only outside, but also in running the 
House. I have been seeing you for the last five years. I have seen hon. Chairman, our 
Vice-President, running the House so pa ssionately. You have been talking of 
legislative improvements and Parliament losing  its relevance if we do not rise to the 
occasion. But one big thing which is troub ling India's parliamentary democracy in the 
75th year of our Republic is the anti-defection. Anti-defection needs to be addressed. 
You need to have the will to curb it. You can do it. Why do you allow the courts to 
intervene on every defection which happens  across the State? The Parliament is 
competent enough. Earlier, we had Committees. Constitute a committee or let the 
Committee on Law and Justice look into the evolution of defections and make certain 
amendments which can tighten anti-defection or make it watertight so that in the 75th 
year India should be a shin ing example as we call ourselves ‘Mother of Democracy’. 
But if we have such lapses, we may not be setting the right example.  So anti-
defection is one area. Under your leadersh ip, I really look forward. Suggestions, 
definitely, would be forthcoming from the Ch air.  So, with these few words, I thank 
you for the opportunity. I once again thank the Rashtrapati ji for addressing us.

SHRI VAIKO (Tamil Nadu): Sir, I thank you very much for giving me the opportunity to

delivered to the Parliament on the 31
st January, 2025. Sir, the time at my disposal is

Machiavelli wrote a book ‘The Prince’. That was the guiding book for Benito 
Mussolini, Adolf Hitler and Idi Amin. There,  he gives three conc epts. One, might is 
right. Whatever we do with might, that is justifiable. Then, necessity knows no laws. If 
there is a necessity, no law is required. The final thing is very important.  Ends justify

[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
the means. That means you can do an ything, the results should be the good .  But

should not do anything illegal or immoral to feed your mother. This  is the concept of 
Tamilians given by Thiruva lluvar about 2,000 years ago. But now I am afraid, the

concept is of one nation. It is a multi-nation al State. There are so many nationalities. I

nation, and one language, Hindi and Sans krit, the country will be balkanized like

bulldoze all the nationalities, all the cultures , all the civilizations and you want to make

Hon. Vice-Chairman, Sir, the President's Address side-steps the real concerns of the

with Odisha’s challenges, including devastat ing cyclones that we face, the critical 
demand was not addressed or reflected in her speech.  The Address is silent on some 
of the most crucial issues from the Stat e where she actually belongs from, Odisha.  
Issues relating to Odisha such as Po lavaram and Mahanadi disputes were not 
reflected in the speech. Non-revision of  coal royalty, lack of green tax 
implementation, inadequate national hi ghways, poor railway connectivity and 
insufficient mobile and digital infrastructure which are so critical for Odisha, the State 
that hon. President belongs to, were not addressed or referred even once.Moreover, 
it ignores the long-standing demand for the inclusion of 139 communities in the 
Schedule Tribe List of Odisha, as well as the inclusion of Ho, Mundari, Bhumij, Kui 
and Saora languages in the Eighth Schedule to the Constitution.   
 Additionally, the hon. President’s Address fails to mention the need for 
complete withdrawal of 18 per cent GST on  Kendu Leaf, a liveli hood source for ten 
lakh tribal women in Odisha.  This has been consistently demanded by our leader Shri 
Naveen Patnaikji.  Sir, I will conclude in 30 seconds.

SHRI AJIT KUMAR BHUYAN (Assam):  Thank you, Vice Chairman, Sir, for giving me 
the opportunity.  Let me start with the so- called affection and love of the Narendra 
Modi Government for tribal people. In the President’s Address, there is no concrete 
response to the incident of stripping tribal women in Manipur. I can flag several issues 
regarding tribal communities where the Modi Government has taken a very casual 
approach. These are a few issues I want to bring to the House's attention.  
Sir, there is silence on the destruction of forests and tribal life in Chhattisgarh. No 
action has been taken yet on the demand fo r the Sixth Schedule in Ladakh. There is 
no objection to taking away tribal land in  the Andaman and Nicobar Islands. There is 
an unconstitutional classification in the Scheduled Castes and Scheduled Tribes 
reservation. The long-pending demand for gr anting SC status to six ethnic groups in 
Assam remains unaddressed. The Naga conf lict, despite a framework agreement in 
2015, has been put on the backburner. 
 Sir, it is painful to say that despit e having Shrimati Droupadi Murmu as the 
President of India, the 10 per cent tribal po pulation has been suffering for no fault of 
theirs. Meanwhile, Modi ji is beating with a high pitch that his Government has done 
[ 04 February, 2025 ]
141

 
 
tremendous work for tribal people, unmat ched by any other Prime Minister in 
independent India.  
 Sir, I am happy that the Government ha s granted Classical Language status to 
Assamese, but the Government is silent on the Sixth clause of the Assam Accord and 
its implementation. The Government's intent  seems doubtful on granting classical 
status to the Assamese language as pe r the Sixth clause of the Assam Accord, 
ensuring protection of Assamese culture, tradition, and language. I quote Gandhiji, 
"Allah ishwar tero naam sabko sanmati de bhagwan."  Talking big has become 
Modiji's normal routine, but nothing concrete has been done for the suffering sections 
of society. An animosity between religion and caste has been created to divert 
attention from basic issues that people face in their day-to-day life. 
 Sir, my previous speakers have co vered issues regarding inflation, 
unemployment, and corruption at the highest level. However, I would like to highlight 
two critical issues: The isolation in the minds of North-East people due to ignoring the 
Manipur conflict and drug trafficking, which is  spoiling the region's youth. I conclude 
with dismay that the Narendra Modi Gove rnment is anti-constitutional, and an 
undeclared emergency has crushed di ssent, differences, and democratic 
disagreement. Thank you, Sir.

SHRI JOSE K. MANI (Kerala) : Thank you, Sir, for giving me this opportunity. I stand 
here to speak on the Motion of Thanks on  the President's Address. But let me be 
clear, this is not a speech of gratitude,  but one of disappointment. The Address 
should have been a mirror, reflecting the struggle of every Indian. But instead, it has 
chosen selective silence. Be it the ongoi ng unrest in Manipur, the devastation in 
Wayanad, the rising human-wildlife conflict or the failure of the Union Budget, the 
Government has conveniently looked on the other way. If this is the Government's 
idea of ‘ Sabka Saath, Sabka Vikas’ , then I must ask who exact ly is included in this 
‘Sabka’ because millions of suffering Indians clearly are not!  
 It conveniently ignored the Central Government's open war against 
Opposition-ruled States. And, make no mistake, this is not governance, this is 
political vendetta. Non-BJP-ruled States like Kerala and many others are being 
144
[ RAJYA SABHA ]

 
 
financially and administratively strangle d simply because they  dare to choose a 
different political path.  
 India's current economic path, which de spite notable GDP growth, is failing to 
generate sufficient employment opportun ities, a phenomenon termed ‘jobless 
growth.’ This has resulted in alarmingly hi gh youth unemployment rates. It is as high 
as 40 per cent. 
  India is facing a deepening crisis of  child substance abuse, I mean, the drug 
abuse. Yet the Government remains shockingly complacent. In December 2022, the 
Government of India admitted before the Supr eme Court that, at least, 1.58 crores of 
children aged between the age-group 10 to 17  are addicted to substance. This is not 
just the statistics; it is a national emer gency. What has the Government done to 
address this issue?  
 Sir, regarding the Railways, the U PA Government focussed on providing 
quality service to the common man, such as the Garib Rath  trains. The current 
Government has diverted resources to build  expensive AC coaches to cater to small 
privileged sections of society. Over the past decade, 33 per cent of the new seats 
added to the Railways have been in AC th ree-tier coaches, even though 95 per cent 
of the passengers travel in ordinary classes or Sleeper class.  Another troubling issue 
is the implementation of fake superfas t surcharges.  The Modi Government has 
labelled about 130 ordinary trains as ‘super fast’ in order to charge passengers an 
extra Rs. 180 per reservation.  Despite this surcharge, these trains do not even meet 
the basic standards of superfast service.  In  fact, CAG found that up to 95 per cent of 
these so-called superfast trains are delayed and did not attain superfast speeds. 
 Sir, another glaring omission in th e President’s Address is the complete 
omission of mention of Kerala, which is gr appling with significant distress resulting 
from the Wayanad landslide and increasing human-wildlife conflicts.  The people of 
Wayanad have endured immense hardship fo llowing the devastating landslide that 
struck last year.  The disaster claimed 375 lives and displaced 10,000 people.  The 
President’s Address overlooked the Wayanad landslide issue and also the escalating 
human-wildlife conflict in Kerala and other parts of India.  This is a grave humanitarian 
crisis in recent times.  Out of the approximately 900 pancha yats in Kerala, nearly 450 
face recurring wildlife attacks.  In the last eight years, this conflict has resulted in 
about 950 deaths. 
 Sir, in conclusion, I stand here not just  to highlight the omission and failure of 
the President’s Address and the Union Budget, but also to call for greater 
accountability from this Government.  The crisis  in Manipur, Kerala and the plight of 
the marginalized and vulnerable sections of our society demand urgent attention.

to stand in Opposition, not just in words but in our struggle for justice, compassion 
and equity for all.  Thank you, Sir.

country in the North East is working.  This has been reflected in the report of Gen. 
S.K. Sinha, who was the Governor of Assa m and also the Governor of Jammu and 
Kashmir. This report was presented to th e President of India by the Governor in 
November, 1998.  Demographic change is a ma jor threat to the people of North East, 
not just Assam. Please ensure that North East does not become another Bangladesh.

ﻟﻴﮑﻦ ﺍﺱ ﮐﺎﻡ ﮐﻮ ﮐﺮﻧﮯ ﻣﻴﮟ ﺳﺮﮐﺎﺭ ﮐﻮ ﺍﻭﺭ ﺧﺎﺹ ﻁﻮﺭ ﺳﮯ ﺍﺗﺮﭘﺮﺩﻳﺶ ﮐﯽ ﺮﮐﺎﺭﺳ ﮐﻮ ﮐﺎﻓﯽ ﮔﻬﻨﭩﮯ ﻟﮓ ﮔﺌﮯ ﺗﻬﮯ ﺍﻭﺭ 
ﺍٓﺝ ﺗﮏ ﺍﺗﺮﭘﺮﺩﻳﺶ ﮐﯽ ﺳﺮﮐﺎﺭ ﺍﺱ ﺣﺎﺩﺛہ ﻣﻴﮟ ﻣﺮﻧﮯ ﻭﺍﻟﻮں ﯽ،
ﮐ ﮨﺘﺎﮨﺖ ﮨﻮﻧﮯ ﻭﺍﻟﻮں ﮐﯽ ﺻﺤﻴﺢ ﺻﺤﻴﺢ ﺗﻌﺪﺍﺩ ﻧﮩﻴﮟ  ﺑﺘﺎﭘﺎﺋﯽ 
ﮨﮯ۔ ﻣﻴﮟ ﭼﺎﮨﻮﻧﮕﺎ ﮐہ ﺍﻥ ﺣﺎﻻﺕ ﺳﮯ ﻧﮑﻠﻨﮯ ﮐﮯ ﻟﻴﮯ ﺳﺮﮐﺎﺭ ﮐﻮ ﮐﻤﺒﻬ ﮐﮯ ﺍﻧﺪﺭ ﺟﻮ ﺣﺎﻻﺕ ﭘﻴﺪﺍ ﮨﻮﺋﮯ ﮨﻴﮟ ﺍﺱ ﮐﯽ ﺫﻣہ 
ﺩﺍﺭی ﻟﻴﻨﯽ ﭼﺎﮨﺌﻴﮯ، ﺟﻦ ﺧﺎﻧﺪﺍﻧﻮں ﮐﺎ ﺟﺎﻧﯽ ﻧﻘﺼﺎﻥ ﮨﻮﺍ ﮨﮯ ﻳﺎ ﺟﻮ ﻟﻮگ ﮔﻬﺎﺋﻞ ﮨﻮﺋﮯ ﮨﻴﮟ، ﺍﻥ ﮐﯽ ﻣﺪﺩ ﮐﺮﻧﯽ ﭼﺎﮨﻴﺌﮯ ﺍﻭﺭ 
ﺍﻣﻮﺍﺕ ﮐﯽ ﺻﺤﻴﺢ ﺻﺤﻴﺢ ﺗﻌﺪﺍﺩ ﺑﺘﺎﻧﯽ ﭼﺎﮨﻴﺌﮯ۔ ﺍﺱ ﺗﻌﺪﺍﺩ ﮐﻮ ﺣﺎﺻﻞ ﮐﺮﻧﮯ ﮐﮯ ﻟﻴﮯ ﺳﺮﮐﺎﺭ ﺍﻳﮏ ﺳﻴﻨﭩﺮ ﺎﺳﮑﺘﯽ
ﺑﻨ ﮨﮯ، 
ﺍﻳﮏ ﻭﻳﺐ ﺳﺎﺋﭧ ﮐﻬﻮﻝ ﺳﮑﺘﯽ ﮨﮯ، ﺍﻳﮏ ﻧﻤﺒﺮ ﺟﺎﺭی ﮐﺮﺳﮑﺘﯽ ﮨﮯ ﮐہ ﺟﻦ ﮐﮯ ﻟﻮگ ﻻﭘﺘہ ﮨﻴﮟ، ﻭﻩ ﺍﺱ ﭘﺮ ﺭﭘﻮﺭٹ ﮐﺮﻳﮟ
